# 06 — Screens & Design

## R1 design of record

- **R1 design of record:** the "Grimoire R1 Final Review" canvas (approved 2026-10-18). Its source files are in `docs/design/r1/`. Where it and these docs differ, the docs' written rules win and the canvas is updated.
- Not drawn but specified: Messaging between the GM and a player (M5 gate B3, 2026-11-01: the ✉ icon, the Messages screen, the silent card; `design/mockups/m5-gate/b3-messaging.html`); Split Loot across several companies (GM sets each company's portion, then each charter applies).
- The GM tablet encounter-table screen is retired; its jobs moved into the GM console (Explore / Encounter / Fight).

## Design direction: Folio

**Folio** is the app's look, in light and dark: a book-page feel with EB Garamond for names, section labels, key numbers and sheet titles, Atkinson Hyperlegible for body text, small-caps labels, double-ruled section dividers, dotted row rules and few boxes. Legibility always wins over flavor. Chosen after five mockup rounds (see the "Grimoire Player screen mockups" canvas in claude.ai). Tokens are below.

## Design principles (ruled 2026-10-24; apply everywhere)

- **Main layouts:** iPad landscape for the GM **and for players** (ruled 2026-11-01, R2: players mostly use iPads; builds test player screens on iPad first), then phone portrait. Both orientations must work on both devices. Where this document says "phone" for a player screen, read "the player's device, iPad first".
- **Never open the keyboard automatically** (ruled 2026-11-01, M5 gate A4), app-wide, unless a spec says otherwise; a field takes focus on a tap.
- **The players' screen during play is a live table view** (ruled 2026-11-01, R3): clock, light, place, marching order and statuses at a glance, with the players **prompted** when they must act (a rest due, a roll, a message); never a menu of things to look for. Narration stays at the table: the app reveals descriptions and visual aids, nothing more.
- **Prominence follows frequency:** everyday actions are big and up front; rare ones sit behind ⋯ or one tap away.
- **No explanations on screen:** put them behind an ⓘ (e.g. a container's "holds things; doesn't add capacity"). **One named exception, guided flows only** (character creation, later level-up and campaign setup; ruled 2026-10-25): one line at the top of each step, about 12 words at most, in Ogham's voice; detail stays behind ⓘ. Play screens keep this principle. No line on a screen explains what the screen already shows; words that carry a fact or a consequence stay (what an answer does, what was logged), and an empty screen may say why it is empty (mc-46, 2026-11-12).
- **A choice becomes a button row only when every option fits one row at Standard text size;** otherwise it's a vertical list (ruled 2026-10-25, clarifying M4 gate 7). None selected shows "—".
- **Any popup or sub-form opens scrolled to its first field.**
- **The primary action on phone sits in a sticky bottom action bar** (ruled 2026-10-25, replacing M4 gate rule 18's "buttons on top" on phone; same intent: always visible, never below the fold): full width, on any screen that has one (forms, sheets, Give, Add, review); it rides above the keyboard, and the field being typed in always scrolls into view. Secondary actions never share the bar. **Tablet keeps the primary action top right.**
- **Undo over confirm** (ruled 2026-10-25): reversible actions happen at once with an Undo toast. Dialogs are only for irreversible or high-cost actions (a character's death, deleting homebrew). **End expedition** is undoable, so it has no confirm: its summary shows with Undo (ruled 2026-10-29, D22, replacing its confirm).
- **After any action on an item, or adding one:** close the item and return to the top of the Pack screen.
- **Changes on one device show on the others without reloading.**
- **Buttons (ruled 2026-10-25):** a soft shadow with the 1.5 px outline kept; secondary buttons carry a Garamond label so they never look like fields; pressed drops 1 px and loses the shadow; **quiet** buttons (underlined accent text in a 44 px tap area) only for going somewhere and help lines, never an action (fl-1, 2026-11-03: any other action is a secondary button, small in a card's foot); pop-ups carry the only deeper shadow.
- **One height in a row** (ruled 2026-11-18, p2p-16, `design/rulings/scene-point-to-point-2026-11-18.md`; app-wide, built screen by screen at the sweep): controls side by side in one row share one height, a button beside a button or an icon button beside a button.
- **Never a disabled button:** a blocked action stays tappable and explains why in a pop-up.
- **Messages are pop-ups; working panels stay in place without moving content** (ruled 2026-10-25). Messages (confirmations, warnings, errors, notices) are a **toast** for information or a **dialog** for a choice, never a box in the page. Working panels (the reminder card, urgent strip, Waiting for you, map panel, a pinned GM note) stay in the page, with their space reserved or sliding over content, so nothing jumps; they collapse when empty, only between actions. **On the Player tab no notice sits in the columns** (tfn-1, 2026-11-19): what is news goes into the **Notices box** at the top of the right column, so the two columns always start level (page M1 approved 2026-11-19, not built; until it is built the urgent strip and "No light" show as before).
- **Toasts with Undo** (ruled 2026-10-25): anything that makes something leave your view (a drop, a gift, emptying a container, the last of a stack) shows one toast above the tab bar for 6 seconds, with a draining bar and **Undo** (the same Undo as the log, with the same rules). Equipping gear that stops casting also toasts ("Chainmail stops arcane casting · Undo"), and so does an HP change from the damage pad (M6-3; it replaced the quick chips). A light burnt out toasts "Torch burnt out · Light another" with no Undo: the clock caused it, so undoing the turn from the log restores it (ruled 2026-10-26). One toast at a time; other actions stay silent, except settings screens (Rules for this campaign, Character fields), which say "Saved" about 1.5 seconds after the last change (ruled 2026-10-26). Wording: Words batch 1, below.
- **No drag handles anywhere** (ruled 2026-10-25, replacing "the handle is on the right"). Hold a row (about half a second) to drag it; tap it to open it. Reorderable lists explain "hold to move" behind an ⓘ.
- **Text size:** layouts are designed and polished at Standard; Large and Larger keep working, untuned.
- **An optional panel with nothing in it collapses,** and its space goes to the other panels (ruled 2026-10-25).
- **Pop-ups and pop-out panels over nested collapsible lists** (ruled 2026-10-30, S4-7): a section that would nest folds inside folds opens as a pop-up or a pop-out panel instead. Reference lists (rules) stay collapsible, with a search bar.

## Accessibility and calm

- **Built in from now on, invisible** (ruled 2026-10-25): screen-reader labels, shapes as well as colours, contrast. **Accessibility mode** (per person, Off by default) arrives in **R3**: Move up / Move down in action sheets, alternatives to gestures, larger targets.

- Sizes come from the type scale (Design tokens): body 16; key numbers 24–36; HP 48 as the screen's single focal number; **nothing below 14**.
- Tap targets ≥44px with 8px between; +/− buttons 48px on phones.
- **Colour and shape** (relaxed 2026-11-12 from "never colour alone", `design/rulings/dec-colour-rule-2026-11-12.md`): colour may carry a distinction by itself where its mockup shows it with the hue taken away, the contrast is measured in both modes, and Francis has approved it; text contrast floors still hold. Signals drawn before that ruling keep their pairing: every such colour signal also has a text label or a shape (tiers, light warnings, statuses; ▲ warning, ✕ danger, ⓘ info). The Scene light and dark-sight cues pair colour with shape (see Navigation).
- High contrast in light and **dark mode** (dark is the default at night/dim rooms).
- **Theme:** light or dark, switched from the settings / ⋯ menu (not a button on every screen). The first run follows the device's own light/dark setting; the person's choice then sticks per device. Automatic switching by ambient light is a later release ◈ (browser support for light sensors is limited; following the device's own light/dark setting is the likely route).
- The theme and text-size switches size each option to its own word and fill their border; no label is cut off at any text size.
- Max one accent-colored button per screen, counted on the surface a person can act on: the page dimmed under a sheet's backdrop is not counted, and a chosen state (a pill's tint and edge) is not an accent (sa-1, 2026-11-15). No badges, counters or animations competing for attention. One exception: the GM's Players tab shows a plain-text count of the items and new characters waiting for the GM ("Players · 2"), only when something is waiting, never as a coloured badge. Any other count needs its own ruling. One ruled motion (GM rail A2, 2026-11-08; plan m5b-sheet-revised PR 2): a GM tab with something to act on draws its icon in the lit-light glow with a slow breathe (2.4 s, a still glow under reduced motion), its name saying what waits ("Players: 1 to approve" on a phone; on a tablet its shown words first, "Players, 3 waiting: 2 to approve, 1 to answer", WCAG 2.5.3, 2026-11-11); it goes out when the tab's list empties, never on a visit.
- No charts beyond simple bars in R1; any chart must be readable at a glance from arm's length.
- **Darkness** (ruled 2026-10-30, DG7; the dim removed at the M5 gate, R3 audit 8, 2026-11-01): when a player's group has no light, the screen shows the reminder "No light · Light a torch (N left)" and an "in darkness" symbol on the time strip and the marching order; the screen itself never darkens or greys, and there is no device switch for it.
- **Tap for detail:** any stat, status, item, light, actor or log entry opens a sheet with its rules reference or a more detailed view. Tappable lines flash on press (at least ~150ms) before the sheet opens; a one-time hint teaches this.
- Every rules popup ends with one small source line naming the pack and section (e.g. "From your setting: River Rules · spells").
- No explanatory subheadings under screen titles. Titles stand alone; context lives in the content or one tap away.

## Layouts

- **Tablet** (primary): two panes, list left and detail right. GM console uses the full landscape width.
- Layout follows the window's width, not the device: **phone** under 600px, **tablet portrait** 600–1023px (one column), **tablet landscape** 1024px and up (two panes). Rotation and iPad Split View pick the matching layout. On a landscape tablet the Player screen sits left of the full sheet, and the GM's Campaign screen uses the left column (sigil, name, sections).
- **Phone**: single column, bottom tab bar (in landscape too), bottom sheets for details.
- **Landscape rule for every list-and-detail screen** (tablet landscape; ruled 2026-10-24): the left column has a minimum width (about a third of the screen), and names end in "…" rather than wrapping; the right side is never empty (the first entry selected, or a summary: for a Pack, the load bar, tier and totals); item details open as a centred card, never a third column. GM **Whose pack**: characters on the left, the chosen character's Pack (as the player sees it) on the right. Player **Pack**: groups and items on the left, the load summary or the open item on the right.
- **Sign-in:** the name and tagline as one centred block a third of the way down, with a rule under the pair; on a phone held upright the block sits near the top instead (not vertically centred), so the name and the email field stay above the keyboard (N1, 2026-10-28).
- **Scene on a tablet or small monitor**: three columns, landscape; details open as a centered card. The log opens from the top row (see Navigation).
- GM console and Scene tablet layouts each have Explore and Fight variants (three columns each).

**Visual, not walls of text (Francis, 2026-11-02, dt-26):** screens stay visually interesting; prefer bars, badges, cards and symbols (always with words beside them) to lines of text. Downtime screens: `design/mockups/downtime/01–07`.

**Test feedback of 2026-11-03** (`design/rulings/m5b-test-feedback-2026-11-03.md`): selected states use the Selected wash with an accent border, ink text and ✓ (tf-4); one Close everywhere, a bare ✕ top right, 44 px, named "Close" (tf-8); rules with up to 3 short choices as a segmented control, more as a ▾ button with "1 of N" (tf-3); the GM reference has a 220 px left list with the headings From the rules · The book's GM pages, no "pack" tag (tf-10). Mockups `design/mockups/library/04`, `05`.

**The full character sheet (ruled 2026-11-04, `design/rulings/m5b-sheet-review-2026-11-04.md`, sr-1 … sr-10):** four captioned tiles (HP with hit dice, AC, Attack with melee and missile, Level with XP); one Movement section in the pack's words; Skills in two groups (everyone's, the class's own); Traits by source; a Magic section (pips per level, a tab per kind); Advancement with an XP bar; a field only when it applies; tablet in two columns (play figures left, who they are right), phone stacked with one-line summaries; Attack's tap with the to-hit row under descending AC; the Pack tab's load as a bar per band. Mockups `design/mockups/sheet/01`, `02`.

## Navigation

**One top row, no top bar (2026-10-23).** The header with the app's name (a small-capitals wordmark) and ⋯ shows only on sign-in and Your campaigns. Every other screen has one top row: a back link on the left, and Log (inside a campaign) and ⋯ on the right; the screen's own title sits below it as before. Inside a campaign the back link at a screen's top level shows the campaign's sigil (small, when it has a picture) and name, "‹ The Ash Barrows", and goes back to Your campaigns; a long name ends in "…" on a phone (screen readers still get the whole name). On the GM's campaign screen the back link reads "‹ Your campaigns" instead, because its big editable heading below carries the sigil and name and is the way into editing the campaign. Sub-screens keep their own back link ("‹ Players", "‹ Back to Wren"). Outside a campaign (Rules library, New campaign, Join) it reads "‹ Your campaigns". The ⋯ holds the screen's own actions first, then, below a divider, the campaign's items, the same on every screen inside the campaign, the character sheet, Setting rules and the list included (N17, ruled 2026-10-28), most used first (the player's campaign items: Your characters, Full character sheet, Company, Setting rules, then This device; ruled 2026-10-25 and 2026-10-26; Arrange this screen is hidden until the Player screen redesign, ruled 2026-10-26. The GM's holds only what isn't a GM tab (ruled 2026-10-26): Company (the Companies section of Players) · Handouts (if not a tab, with a quiet "N new" count; from M8b, when Handouts arrive; ruled 2026-10-29, D26) · This device; Players' characters and Rules are tabs, and the Players tab carries the "N waiting" badge. End expedition stays in the time strip's ⋯), with no wasted space, then This device (theme, text size), Rules library and Sign out, so everything the old header offered is on every screen. Each option has a symbol in the accent colour beside its words (never instead of them; placeholders until the Style release's symbol family), in 48 px rows with no gaps, and This device sits below a thin divider (ruled 2026-10-26). The row stays at the top as you scroll (see Top row below).

**The phone's navigation (N1, ruled C on 2026-11-06; mockup `creation/17-phone-tabs-and-drawer.html`; built as slice E, #549, 2026-11-07).** Under 600 px: a **bottom tab bar** with four tabs, Player, Scene, Pack, Log for players (icons a crown, a campfire, a tied sack, an open book) and Scene, Players, Rules, Log for the GM (Rules takes the scales on every device; the open book is Log everywhere); the current tab bold with a 3 px accent mark above it, never colour alone; a badge (14 px) only for what waits (the GM's unread messages on Scene, "N waiting" on the GM's Players; nothing at 0, "99+" past 99), and the words in the tab's label for a screen reader. Above, one **top bar**: ≡ at the left opens a **drawer** (the campaign's name and, for the GM, a gear to Rules for this campaign; the groups This campaign, Your characters, As the GM, Elsewhere; the signed-in person at the foot with This device, Rules library and Sign out; built from the ⋯'s groups, not copies; a player with no character sees no Full character sheet or Company row), the title centred (the character's name on Player and "<name>'s pack" on Pack, the campaign's on Scene), ⋯ at the right trimmed to the screen's own options and hidden when there are none. The tab bar hides inside creation and other flows, where the ← bar takes over (one bar at any width that shows a top row instead of the rail; final draft 9). The tablet keeps its rail and top tabs. The lines "no badges" and "the same icons on phone and tablet" above this date are superseded by this paragraph.

**Same on every device:** each role sees the same sections, in the same order, with the same icons on phone and tablet. Phone shows them as bottom tabs, tablet portrait as top tabs, tablet landscape as the left column, which can collapse to icons (the **divider is the handle**: a quiet pill at mid-height, the divider a 44 px tap zone, tap or drag; nothing in the column, no words; remembered per device — ruled 2026-11-02, lr-4, `design/mockups/library/02-menu-fold.html`, superseding B15's chevron row at the foot; starts folded (mn-1, 2026-11-14)). Folded, the GM's column keeps the campaign's sigil at its top, a 44 px tap named with the campaign that opens the menu, and for a retired campaign a **Retired** mark under it (the lock and the word, in the warning colour) whose tap opens a small pop-up, "Retired campaign", with the banner's words and **Bring back** (mn-2, ap-17, mockup `design/mockups/december/16-the-scenes-top-in-every-state.html`, frame T13). Groupings may differ between GM and players.
- **Players:** Player · Scene · Pack, and **Magic** for a character with any magic (mp-20, ruled 2026-11-05; built in M5c).
- **GM:** Scene · Players · Rules. The GM's campaign opens on **Scene** (the console is the GM's home), with Players one tap away (ruled 2026-10-29; players' devices are unchanged). Players holds players and characters (the tab reads "Players · N waiting" when something waits, ruled 2026-10-26; the Waiting for you section shows only then, at the top of the GM's sections), Companies and **Inventory** (the Party stash first, then "Whose pack"; renamed 2026-10-24; the player's tab stays Pack). There is no More tab: ⋯ holds This device, Rules library and Sign out. Until M5 builds Scene, the GM sees Players · Rules.
- **Log:** a log button in the top row, beside ⋯, for everyone. It opens the log full screen on phone and iPad (ruled 2026-10-24; no side panel); the sheet follows the finger and closes on a swipe down from near the top. A note's unsaved draft survives closing. Scene keeps a few recent log lines, with no full-log link.
- **Top row:** stays at the top as you scroll, with the back link, Log and ⋯ (tap targets at least 44px); the screen title sits below it and scrolls away. From the M5 gate (2026-11-01) the GM's top row also carries **Messages** (✉, with the unread count; B3) and **Reference** (an open book; B4); players get ✉ only.
- **Icons:** one family for the whole app; the same thing gets the same icon on every screen (Scene campfire, Players people, Rules book, Log notes, Player person, Pack backpack). Placeholders now; the carved family replaces them all at once in the style release.

Mic (dictation) sits on text fields.

A **Company** page opens from the party sigil at the top of Scene (until M5, from the player's ⋯ menu). It holds the charter (with members and titles), Treasury, hirelings and holdings. A player whose character has no company sees **Found a company** and **Ask to join** there together (ruled 2026-10-24). With companies switched off for the campaign, there's no Company page. The tabs stay Player · Scene · Pack.

### Players — Adventure mode
- **Player** (redesigned, ruled 2026-10-26; 05): header (name 30, "Class level, Race" (plan m5b-players-screens-standard B: the name in the bar, the statuses as chips under this line) with race-as-class showing the class's race, ruled 2026-10-29, status symbols right); the avatar (initial or portrait) is the tap for the portrait sheet (ruled 2026-11-08), **96 px** (frame P1's was 64), the four status rings beside it as before (ps-5), and the **Notices box** at the top of the right column over Scores (page M1, approved 2026-11-19; not built; see Notices box); vitals (HP largest with the segmented bar and "of N", changed in place: − and + at the right of the figure, each tap at once, taps within two seconds one logged change with one Undo, the figure's tap types a value; the Damage or heal pop-up left the Player screen, ruled 2026-11-08, mockup players/04 C; with no max recorded, the number and a small "Set max" button (fl-1), no bar, N24; AC, always at the top, the ability modifier included (the setting's unarmoured value plus the modifier with nothing worn, N22; "—" and a quiet "Set AC" link when nothing gives a value, B9; a set-by-hand AC shows its number, and its breakdown reads "Set by hand" with Clear, ruled 2026-10-29), and attack, one quiet line each, a tap explains with long names, N23); then the weapon in hand as its own block (dec-13 C14), Light and At hand, the level block and Details (no Spells section: a caster's spells are on the Magic tab, mp-20, built dec-8 B; coin is on the Pack tab). **Two columns from 1024 px** (dec-13 C1, ps-2): the left as above, scrolling on its own; the right Scores, Saves, Movement and load, Traits (with uses), Skills, Languages, then the rest of the full sheet under "Full character sheet" with Edit (the heading at the row names' size, `--text-20`, as frame P1 draws it; Francis's fix of 19 Nov 2026; `.player__right .sheet__pane-title`, `PlayerScreen.css`; the full sheet's own page keeps its page heading), each the full sheet's own part, hosted, and nothing shown twice. **Ruled 2026-11-19 (tfr-4, tfr-5; page M1 approved, not built):** this tab has no "Full character sheet" heading and no Edit; the rows under it (Weapon skills, Abilities, Traits, Patron, Magic, Notes) stay where they are; Edit is on the cards of "Your characters" that are approved or waiting, not on a sent-back or an archived one (05). Under every score its modifier always shows, "+0" for none, and a score with a change waiting keeps its modifier and says nothing of it: the change is in the Notices box only (tfr-3). On a phone the head is the portrait, the head's line and the box under it, the rings on their own row. **One column below** (ps-2a): the saves directly under hit points and AC, then the left's order, then the right's, and "Full character sheet" a row with a chevron last (plan m5b-players-screens-standard A2; from tfr-5 those rows themselves take its place). No party list and no marching order on this screen (ps-4); no hint line (mc-46). In the book's type (the shared BookType scope), every row's name in the book's bold (the List row's **row name, bold** variant). **One loading state** (dec-13 C11): until the setting rules, the play state, the sheet and the pack have each answered, the Player tab shows one "Opening…" and none of its parts (each mounted, its read running); if the rules or the play state can't be read, one error with Try again; afterwards a read again never blanks the screen. On the device's stored copy of the rules, the offline line above the head ("Setting rules as of <date>, offline"). See **Player screen states**.
- **Scene**: the shared view (see `05-features.md`): follows the GM's mode; time, initiative, foes, the party in marching order (drag to reorder), a few recent log lines (the full log opens from the top row). In an adventure (M8b): the open location's player view (its player name, or "Unknown place"), day or night, and a **Handouts** button; each reveal shows one quiet card (silent, and shown at once even in quiet mode), then joins Handouts. Handouts shows two groups: **From your adventure** and **Your uploads**.
- Scene: light and dark sight use shape plus colour: a glowing ring with a flame mark for a lit light, a dashed ring with an eye mark for dark sight. No text labels; tap for details; screen readers announce both.
- **Pack**: inventory grouped by where it's carried, A–Z within each group; counts only on stackables; tap an item for its sheet (details, Give to…, Put in…, the Pin star, Drop last; on a stack, then how many); Add item, the screen's one accent button, in the foot with the count (plan m5b-players-screens-standard C). An **Other** section (temporary name; animals, tack, vehicles, boats: 07 `separateCategories`) sits apart, outside the load, hidden when empty. A container's sheet has **Empty…**. A player's waiting one-offs show as a card with the warning sign, "N items waiting for the GM", that opens their list as a sheet. At hand (first, the pinned items, each listed only there; Design on #552) and "Worn and in hand" are always open; the groups that fold (Gear with armour encumbrance, the Party stash below a pack, as fold rows with a one-line summary) start collapsed when the character has something At hand, and with nothing At hand Gear starts open (N19, ruled 2026-10-28, replacing 2026-10-26); once someone opens or closes one, that device remembers how it was left. The **Party stash** has no groups but its containers: its loose things in one plain list, then "In the {container}" for each container in it (no Worn, Carried, Gear or Other; N18, ruled 2026-10-28).
- **Dungeon on Scene: the players' table view** (M5; ruled 2026-10-30, DG5, DG7–DG10; redesigned at the M5 gate, B18, ruled 2026-11-01; mockup in `design/mockups/index.md`): one sheet in three bands, top to bottom — the **time band** (mode and turn in the display size, tap for the procedure, B5; the rest countdown, the player's own light, Day / Night, date, weather as a word and symbol; **in darkness**: when the player's group has no light the light's slot reads **"◑ No light"**, symbol and words, tap for the rule — the "in darkness" symbol the R3 audit kept, designed 2026-11-01 on the builder's question; the GM's strip shows it per group); the **marching order** as tiles front to back (the player's own tile outlined in ink; lights and statuses in words beside their symbols; a group in darkness reads **"◑ No light"** on each tile, symbol and words, ruled 2026-11-03, SC-g6-2; tap a tile for its statuses and effects; a long-press or the full order to drag, ~~the GM's lock applies~~ no lock (p2p-21, ruled 2026-11-18; the setting "Players can reorder marching order" decides); one per own group in a split party) with the **Rest** button under it (calls a rest, B5); the **place** (its name and the shown pieces in the order the GM shows them, B1, nothing else about the place; on iPad the order stays in view when the place overflows). Below the place, and only there (ruled 2026-11-03, SC-g6-1): the hour track with its public events, then a few recent log lines (05, Scene tab); the procedure reference in the players' words opens from the time band (tap the mode and turn). The steady state has nothing else: no buttons to look for and no accent colour. **Everything is tappable for its explanation**: a mark, a tile, a status, a piece opens a short card in the pack's words. **Prompts** are cards in one slot between the time band and the marching order, space reserved: the rest prompt (Rest / Push on / ⓘ, B5), a message ("✉ Message from the GM · Open", B3), the weather roll, a request's answer, an escape roll, "No light · Light a torch (N left)" (the darkness dim is gone, R3 audit 8; the in-darkness symbol stays on the time band and the tiles); newest on top (what is there when the screen opens keeps a fixed order: rest, a request, a message, No light, the weather; a prompt that arrives later goes above, ruled 2026-11-03, SC-g6-4), each going when answered; only a prompt carries the screen's one accent button. Something else… is gone (A5). Phone: the same bands stacked, tiles two per row.
- **Wilderness on Scene** (M5b; ruled 2026-10-30, D1, D3–D5, D14, D18, S4-1 to S4-5; 05, The players' wilderness screen), top to bottom: the date with the weather icon (tap for details; the weather card, Enter roll when players roll it, also in the Player screen's urgent strip, shows the result and its effects at once and folds into the icon on a tap; S2-1, A8; the 8-second fold dropped at the M5 gate, R3 audit 15); travel points left (the weather change only when it isn't zero); the ground around them (terrain always, a place's name only once shown); the marching strip with no "You" tag (ds-12, 2026-11-12); the rest prompt when a rest day is due, with **Rest today** (answers after #170); then **one button group**: the pack's activities by their own names (a secret one as a request showing its cost, "Search the area · 3 travel points", S4-3), and **Force march** with ⓘ, laid out evenly by count (2 columns on phone, 3 on tablet, an odd last button centred); then the **What we know** card, which pops out into a panel (the places the GM has shown, with their descriptions, and revealed maps; handouts from M8b). No **Something else…** (removed at the M5 gate, A5; the pack's common player actions go to the GM reference screen, B4). **During camp** the middle of the screen (between the strip and What we know) swaps to the camp steps in the pack's order (Eat, your watch, Campfire talk, cooking, sleep; S4-5). The location view opens as a sheet on a phone.
- **Town on Scene** (M5b; ruled 2026-10-30, settlement dry run, ST3, ST5–ST9): the town screen's player version (only the places and sections the GM shows); **Ask for…** (the town's list or **Something else…**), **Sell…** (item, then buyer) with the GM's **Offer** to **Accept** or **Decline**, the lodgings with their rates, and the town activities with each rule one tap away. Each morning, the **overnight summary** card (HP healed and why, statuses changed, the lodging cost).
- **Reveal** (GM, on an unidentified item, M4b): a popup listing each hidden fact in plain words, plus Tell them everything.
- **About…** (log notes): a small link under the note box; one-tap name buttons for the party's characters; the chosen one as a chip.

### Players — Downtime mode
Same three tabs.
- **Player**: full sheet, XP history, level-up entry, purse history, companions, downtime threads.
- **Company page** (from the sigil on Scene): charter, members and titles, Treasury, hirelings, holdings.

### GM — Adventure console (tablet landscape, one screen)
- Two modes, **Explore** and **Encounter**, switched with one tap; **Fight** is a step inside an encounter (see `05-features.md`).
- **Explore:** time strip (rounds/turns/hours/days read from one clock; one big main advance per mode: **Next turn**, or the mode's own step in a day-by-day mode (Next hour, Next day), long-press for +N; undo; **Advance by…** in ⋯ for odd amounts (runs the procedure for each step) and, since the M5 gate (B12, 2026-11-01), for genuine corrections too (a chosen time or a step back; Set time… folded into it, D19 refined); moving backwards, its sheet adds one line: "Burnt-out lights and ended statuses stay gone. Use Undo to reverse a move exactly." (ruled 2026-10-29); rounds only in a fight; **Start session** while no session runs, D20; until the calendar start is set, the strip reads "Day N" with a quiet **Set date ›** for the GM, ruled 2026-10-29; a **Day / Night** switch in an adventure), reminders and checks (Hit / Miss), marching order (drag; ~~plus a Lock toggle by fiat~~ no lock, p2p-21, ruled 2026-11-18) with light sources and refills, current location, the scene's **Encounter tables** row ("Encounter tables · Abbey likely · 1 pinned ›"; opens the full-screen Which table? list, M5 gate B2, 2026-11-01, replacing the one-row "current table · Roll" of 2026-10-29), and **Table library ›** (the table library, and the way into the prep space). In an adventure (M8b): **Travel** (the route's time, editable before confirming), **Check now**, and the **active check** line with its source ("Abbey · 1–2 on d6 each turn · from the adventure", or "Every 2 turns · yours" when the GM's own reminder is active).
- **Explore, exploration modes (ruled 2026-10-25):** the procedure strip is **one row that never wraps** (a symbol per step; the current one glows with its name; finished ones fade; extra steps scroll), with the full procedure one tap away (tap the mode and turn on the strip, "Dungeon · turn 14", the same for GM and players; ruled 2026-11-01 with B5, replacing the checklist button in the top row) and note steps as one quiet line under the time strip. Prompts show the chance with modifiers counted and each modifier's source, with **+ Modifier** (a sheet with −1 / +1 chips and an optional note; one tap applies, ruled 2026-10-29). A secret check's result has a quiet **Reveal to players**. In day-by-day modes (M5b): the travel budget, an activities row showing only what's usable, and camp from the time strip's ⋯.
- **Hour track (X-1, ruled 2026-10-25):** in turn-by-turn modes, one row of pips for the turns in the hour, the current one filled, with symbols on future pips for party events (checks, rest, effects ending, the first light to run out); the due pip glows. **Next turn** is always on the Explore screen. Turn modes have no activities row: **Act…** moved to the parking lot (ruled 2026-10-30, DG12).
- **Compact time strip** (ruled 2026-10-30, DG2): during an adventure, the pips and **Next turn** show on **every GM screen** (Players, Rules, a fight…), not only Explore.
- **M5 gate small items (ruled 2026-11-01, B7–B17; 05 has the behaviour):** the paused or ended session collapses behind **Resume session** (B7); due marks mean the turn being played (B8); **+ Reminder** and one icon per reminder to the right of the turn area (B9); **Keep lit** is a pressed toggle with ⓘ beside Put out (B10); icons replace the N, M, R letters and every ⋯ item has one (B11); ⋯ reads **Encounter check** and **Advance by…** (Set time… folded in; B12); the start time and a day-and-month date without a pack calendar (B13; the start time sits in Rules for this campaign's Time and calendar group, shown for every campaign: Hour and Minute and **Set start time** while the clock hasn't moved, then a line saying it only shows and pointing to Advance by… → To a time; while the clock hasn't moved, a quiet **Set start time ›** under the GM's date on the full strip opens the same form in a "Start time" pop-up, ruled 2026-11-01); procedure steps in plain words, opened from the strip (B14); the left-hand menu collapses to icons on iPad landscape (B15); the place card drops "Only you see this" and has an expandable **Notes** (B16); no "Revealed this downtime" card on Scene (B17).
- **Dungeon play (M5; ruled 2026-10-30, DG1, DG3, DG11, DG14, DG17, DG18):** **Start expedition** asks the mode and an optional **Where?** (recent places as chips, **Choose from adventures ›** site → level → room, or a typed name). Entering a room opens its **room GM layer**: its **pieces** (description, exits, people, clues, treasure, hazards), each with a **44 × 44 eye button** at the row's right that reaches the group in focus (M5 gate B1, ruled 2026-11-01; open eye + pressed look = shown, struck-through eye = hidden; no word, no note; **+ Piece**, **Hide all**, Undo), the checks it triggers, and the GM's notes on traps, puzzles, encounters, treasure and doors (the GM screen button moved to the GM reference screen, B4). **Encounter check** (named Check now until the M5 gate, B12) sits on the check marker and in ⋯. A split party shows a **group switcher** (one chip per group, the one in focus marked in words, not colour alone; **Join up** in its ⋯); Next turn advances every group. **Leave the dungeon** is a quick button when the pack has an exit rule; its escape rolls come in from players, and the GM previews each outcome (changeable) before **Send**.
- **Wilderness day (M5b; ruled 2026-10-30, S1–S7, D1, D11):** on a landscape tablet the day controls sit on the left (time strip, weather card, budget card, Move…, activities, camp) and the **location view** and marching order on the right; on a phone the location view opens as a sheet. **Move…** (GM) picks the route, terrain and an optional place name and shows the cost and check chances, with Undo in the toast; **Where?** stays for free jumps. The **budget card** replaces the budget line. The **weather card** folds to an icon beside the date (tap to reopen). A check hit queues an **Encounter waiting** card; check prompts show only the rolling instructions and Enter roll (D2). Built in M5b step 1 and its follow-up: the time strip, travel card and camp form the left column and the place card and marching order the right from 1024 px (`PartyGroups`'s `groups-layout--day`, the time strip passed in as `lead`); narrower, they stack in that order. A move's `alsoWhen` checks go on the time strip's card as Hit / Miss lines ("<check> · Left the Causeway"), and the move's Undo takes them off (`moveCheckLines`, `sceneCard.tsx`). On a phone (under 600 px) the place is an Open card, the place's name and one summary line, whose tap opens the location view as a sheet (the Dialog's Wide variant, ✕ top right); the empty card reads "Where are we? · Set place" and its tap is Set place (bq-4, 2026-11-03).
- **Settlement (M5b; ruled 2026-10-30, settlement dry run, ST1–ST5, ST9–ST12):** arriving at a settlement place: **Back in safety** (Safe, the default) runs Split loot and Award XP (M7/M7b) before the town screen; **Into town** (Hostile) opens the town screen only; Safe / Hostile is the GM's setting on the place. The **town screen** is the settlement version of the location view: Overview; Places in town (shops, lodgings, temples, others, each with **Show players** and **Open / Closed**; on a phone a place's name wraps above its two controls); People; Things to do; Encounters (GM-only); Rumours. The **Out and about today** and **Active tonight** switches sit with it. The main advance is **Next part of day** (morning, afternoon, night). Buying requests land in **Waiting for you**: the availability chance as Hit / Miss, then **In stock** · **Not here** · **Price…**. **Start downtime** and **End downtime** switch the campaign's mode.
- **Wilderness day (M5b; ruled 2026-10-30, S1–S7, D1, D11):** on a landscape tablet the day controls sit on the left (time strip, weather card, budget card, Move…, activities, camp) and the **location view** and marching order on the right; on a phone the location view opens as a sheet. **Move…** (GM) picks the route, terrain and an optional place name and shows the cost and check chances, with Undo in the toast; **Where?** stays for free jumps. The **budget card** replaces the budget line. The **weather card** folds to an icon beside the date (tap to reopen). A check hit queues an **Encounter waiting** card; check prompts show only the rolling instructions and Enter roll (D2). Built in M5b step 1 and its follow-up: the time strip, travel card and camp form the left column and the place card and marching order the right from 1024 px (`PartyGroups`'s `groups-layout--day`, the time strip passed in as `lead`); narrower, they stack in that order. A move's `alsoWhen` checks go on the time strip's card as Hit / Miss lines ("<check> · Left the Causeway"), and the move's Undo takes them off (`moveCheckLines`, `sceneCard.tsx`). The phone sheet for the location view is pending.
- **Settlement (M5b; ruled 2026-10-30, settlement dry run, ST1–ST5, ST9–ST12):** arriving at a Safe place (by Where now? or Move…) puts the GM's **arrival card** on the strip (dt-9: End expedition · Not now; then Start downtime · Not yet on the summary); a Hostile place raises none; Safe / Hostile is the GM's setting on the place card (M5b step 6b). The **town screen** is the settlement version of the location view: Overview; Places in town (shops, lodgings, temples, others, each with **Show players** and **Open / Closed**; on a phone a place's name wraps above its two controls); People; Things to do; Encounters (GM-only); Rumours. The **Out and about today** and **Active tonight** switches sit with it. The main advance is **Next part of day** (morning, afternoon, night). Buying requests land in **Waiting for you**: the availability chance as Hit / Miss, then **In stock** · **Not here** · **Price…**. **Start downtime** and **End downtime** switch the campaign's mode.
- **Map panel (ruled 2026-10-25):** on iPad landscape, Explore in every mode has a map or picture panel for the current location (or its region or site); tap it for full screen with pinch to zoom. On phone and iPad portrait, a **Map** chip on the time strip, only when a picture exists. With nothing to show, the panel collapses and the other panels take its space. M5 builds the panel into the layout; M8b fills it from adventure images; players see only revealed copies.
- **Encounter** (M6-7, ruled 2026-10-26): the pack's encounter steps top to bottom (Surprise, Distance, Reaction by default), each with its odds and modifiers (reviewed 2026-11-01, `design/rulings/m6-fight-screens-review-2026-11-01.md`: no Skip on Surprise or Distance; **No fight · back to Explore** opens a small prompt for an optional note, Only you or In the log, Done, Undo); the foes' nudges and an optional **+ Objective** line under the title; surprise as **Foes surprised / Not surprised** (and the party's side when it can be surprised); distance as a number field **defaulting to the average** of the pack's dice, changed by a tap; reaction as a **Roll reaction ›** button that opens the pack's reaction table full screen (B2's screen, modifiers counted), the tapped row filling the line, with ⓘ (what each result means, from the pack); the outcomes **Fight · Talk · Avoid**, all three always there whatever the reaction says (ruled 2026-10-29). After Talk or Avoid, optional detail chips (Bribed, Parleyed, Sneaked past…) show for the log; never required, they disappear if ignored, and the log says "Talked" or "Avoided" unless one is tapped (ruled 2026-10-29).
- **Fight:** see Fight layouts (M6-8) below; the fight's controls column holds the Encounter tables › row (B2) where the encounter-table panel was.
- When the initiative range is small (e.g. 1–6) in individual mode, the entry pad shows one big button per value.
- Quick log line; **Fiat** button; mode switch; Call a vote.
- The Fiat button is a **pointed wizard hat** icon (accent background, labelled "Fiat" for screen readers and on long-press). No text on the button.

### The full character sheet (CS-1..CS-9, ruled 2026-10-25; built after the M4b gate)
- **Layout (B20, ruled 2026-10-25; may iterate):** a compact grid, **Combat first, then Abilities**. Tablet: two self-balancing columns, with the full-width blocks (class abilities, spells) below. Phone: one column of compact grids. Values sit with their labels, never pushed to the far edge. ◈ Arranging the sheet comes later.
- Details the setting fills in sit under their own block (each ability, HP, saves), one tap away; there's no separate "From your setting" heading (ruled 2026-10-25).
- **Identity block, in this order:** name, title, race and class, level, alignment, then trait fields.
- **Abilities** with short captions (full sheet only; the device switch "Ability captions", default On); tap a score for its popup; rule lines behind an ⓘ on the heading.
- **Saves block:** the saves, then the save modifier as a last box set apart with its tag.
- **Combat block:** HP (largest), AC, one attack figure, and a sub-line of each kind's total ("Melee +2, Missile +1"; worked from the figure set by hand when the player has typed a whole number that differs from the class's, H1, 2026-11-19); the to-hit row and the AC breakdown are behind taps.
- **Skills block:** everyone's skills, then class skills; then **Weapon skills** when the setting's `weaponProficiency` option is on (ruled 2026-11-01, `design/rulings/m5b-0-weapon-skills-and-traits-2026-11-01.md`): one row "Weapon skills · sword, spear" (a specialised one marked "· specialised"), ⓘ with the rule and penalty; the owner may **add** a pick while any are unspent (`allowed = start + floor((level − 1) / gainEvery)`, logged), removing or swapping one or changing a specialisation is the GM's (fiat, logged); a level that grants a pick puts "Weapon skills · N to pick" on the To finish card, which reopens the step; the Attack sheet's rolls count it (as-4, 2026-11-10): a part row "Specialised" in the to-hit and damage cards, and in the to-hit card only "Not a weapon skill of yours" or "Not your class’s weapon" (the penalty, kept when the GM approved the exception; specialised in a weapon outside the class, both rows, netted, as-7), and the Weapon skills row says the penalty beside such a pick ("Bog sling (not your class’s weapon, usable at −2 to hit)", as-8), the same totals in At hand and the item sheet; each pack's skills in its own style ("2-in-6" or "5+"), and the tap popup gives the odds both ways ("Succeeds on 5 or 6 · 2 in 6"; CS-5a).
- **Pack summary:** "Equipped 6 / 8 · Stowed 12 / 14 · 60 ft" (invented figures; the words Equipped, Stowed and items are the same on the full sheet's Pack load, pl-1, 2026-11-05), with Tiny as a collapsed section.
- **Languages block:** the languages, Literate or Illiterate, and a quiet "N more to choose".
- **Spells** (M7): one line each (name · duration · range · note), with a header per level ("Level 1 · 2 a day", no count left; M7-3). See Spells (M7) below.
- **To finish** card (M7-4): at the top of the sheet while creation steps wait (see Character creation, guided).

### The character overview (one component, reused everywhere; ruled 2026-10-25)
- Tap a character (marching order, the party, Scene) for one popup: HP, load and speed, light and time left, every status with its source and how it ends, items at hand. It shows only what that viewer may see (HP per "Show each other's HP", renamed 2026-10-30). Keep lit sits here as well as under the light in At hand (A1; as it is, lt-9).

### Marching order (M5-6)
- A column of rows, each 1–3 characters side by side. Each character shows its **light symbol first**, then **up to 2 status symbols**, then **"+N"**, with filling or draining rings. Hold a row to move it; tap a character for the overview.
- **Players' compact strip** (ruled 2026-10-30, DG8): one row of the characters' symbols, front to back; a tap opens the full order to drag (the GM's lock applies). In a split party each group has its own order.

### The reminder card (M5-5)
- One card for what's due: procedure steps, then lights, then rest, then the GM's reminders. **4 lines, then "N more".** Decision lines keep their buttons; information lines have none and go to the log on the next Next or on close. **All done** clears what needs no decision.

### Status symbols (all statuses, from M5; ruled 2026-10-25)
- Each status is a **symbol** (its condition's icon from the app's fixed list, or its first letter in a circle). A timer **fills or drains** the symbol like a cooldown; **stacks** show as a small number badge. Tap for details: name, source, rules text, time left, how it ends or is cured, stacks, and Remove for whoever may. Hidden timers: players see the symbol without the fill. Shape and symbol first, never colour alone; screen readers announce "Exhausted, 2 stacks". Shown on the character card, the Player screen beside HP, and the combat list (M6).

### GM — Location browser and prep mode (tablet, M8b)
- **Location browser:** region → site → room, with search by key or name and a "recently visited" list; a location shows its GM layer, with **Open for the party** (edit what players notice first, if wanted) and **Show players** on anything with an image.
- **Prep mode** (widening into the GM prep space, ruled 2026-10-29; its screens are designed in the G-05 session, 11; outside prep, GM tables stay one tap away but not front and centre): the adventure's locations in page order; each location's summary, clues and player view beside its crop, with inline editing and a **Reviewed** mark. Reuses the location browser's screens.

### Character creation and review (M4b, ruled 2026-10-25)
- **Shopping** at creation (a kit or single items) shows **Gold left: N** at the top; going over shows a calm warning and never blocks.
- **The GM's review** of a new character shows its class, race, scores with the roll where adjusted ("16 (rolled 14)"; a score not rolled carries none, and the line under them reads "Nimbleness: the average, not rolled" or "All six: the average, not rolled", caf-7, caf-8, 2026-11-24, approved from pictures of the real screen after the build), gear, "Over by N gp" when the shopping went over, and a **Needs special approval** flag on any race or class the scores don't meet or that isn't open to the race (ruled 2026-11-02, `design/rulings/m5b-0c-creation-amendments-2026-11-02.md`: the player picked it with a note instead of asking); **Ask for a different level** sits beside Approve and Send back, Approve and Send back both plain (cs-25, 2026-11-12); each typed Other… (a language, a trait entry, a weapon skill: ruled 2026-11-02, built in PR G) is listed with **Add to homebrew** (cf-6, 2026-11-02); a typed weapon becomes a homebrew weapon item with an empty `weapon` block for the GM to fill in. A weapon skill outside the class says what allowing it costs under the pack's reason, "If you allow it: usable at −2 to hit." (as-8, 2026-11-10), and a specialisation in one says so after the weapon's name, "Bog sling, to specialise in: <the pack's reason>" (Design, 2026-11-10).
- **Which way?** is two choice cards (Guided, Freeform). Races and classes are picked on the **list-and-detail** template: on a tablet, the list on the left and the highlighted option's detail on the right with **Choose** (on a landscape tablet across the full width, list about 40% and detail about 60%; ruled 2026-10-26; the detail stays in view beside a long list, and picking an option far down brings it into view, N28, 2026-10-28); on a phone, option rows (name, one-line summary, prime-requisite marks) and a detail sheet. Options the scores or rules don't allow stay visible and pickable at full contrast, never greyed or tinted, with the note "Needs the GM's special approval"; **Choose** picks them all the same, and the GM's review card flags the pick with the requirement it misses (ruled 2026-11-02, replacing the ▲ reason line and the explaining pop-up of 2026-10-25). The heading reads "<Race> <Class>" (B17, ruled 2026-10-25).
- **Kit card** (B17; a choice of kits, ruled 2026-10-26): one card per kit, full width on a phone: the kit's name, "Costs N gp · leaves N gp" (or ▲ "Over by N gp"; a kit with no price has no price line, never "Free", follow-up E 2026-11-06), the first few things in it on one line with **Show all N** below (grouped by where they'll land: "Worn and held", "Carried", "In the backpack"; ruled 2026-10-26), a ▲ line for anything that won't be worn or held as expected and why, a rolled kit's rolls ("Roll for Armour (d6), Weapons (d6, twice).") and anything named in words ("Also: …"), and **Take this kit**. A rolled kit's Take this kit opens one option list per roll ("Armour: what did you roll on d6?"), then **Add to the pack** (it says which roll still needs a tap) and **Not now**. A taken kit says "✓ Taken: it's in the pack below." What was bought is listed once, below the cards, each line removable before approval.
- **Marks** (ruled 2026-10-25): ▲ replaces "!" everywhere (not-allowed rows, blocked destinations, broken rules). Inside a flagged box only the title line carries ▲; its lines start "Broken rule:" in the warning colour. Flagged review sections keep their title first, with the warning sign and "Your GM asks for a change" on the next line; only flagged sections stand out.
- **The review screen** (B18, ruled 2026-10-25): one screen for the player's first submission, a sent-back character and the GM's review card. Sections in reading order (scores shown once: the fields holding them stay out of Details, N12, 2026-10-28; overrides and broken rules marked, each special-approval pick flagged with the requirement it misses (ruled 2026-11-02; on the GM's card too), gear, starting money; no section is tinted, gallery review 2), each jumping to its step. **Send to the GM** is on this screen, after the gear. A sent-back character opens full screen on it, with the GM's note as a pinned working panel until it's resent; sections the GM ticked when sending back are marked. **Resend** stays tappable and explains in a pop-up what's still needed.
- Filled values show under their own block, one tap away; no "From your setting" heading (ruled 2026-10-25).

### Character creation, guided (M5b, pulled forward from M7-4 at the M5 gate, ruled 2026-11-01, R1; first spells moved into m5b-0c on 2026-11-01, cr-25)
- **Each step:** the step counter (this character's steps only; no total before the class is picked, "Step 3", then "Step 4 of N" from the step after the pick, N holding after that: ruled B 2026-11-05), the why-line as the **Guided-flow instruction** (the pack's `creationGuide`, else the app's), a quiet **Help me choose** link that expands the pack's guidance in place (without any, the app's own one-paragraph text for the step: cw-4, 2026-11-06, replacing the choices' blurbs side by side of 2026-11-01), the choices, then **Back** and **Next** on one row (N8). Next stays tappable and says what's missing (N10); Back on the first step leaves (no Later button, N9). Steps that offer it end with a quiet **Decide later**.
- **Choices** are **Option rows, blurb variant**: the name, the pack's one-line `blurb` under it at 14 px (rows 56 px), or a spell's duration and range when there's no blurb; tapping the name opens the full rules in the **Detail sheet**. Classes and races keep the list-and-detail template.
- **First spells:** the **Spell picker** (choose N, given spells shown as chosen, or the roll's rows with their ranges, one tap per table rolled; the rows come from the class's table(s), which the pack marks `gmOnly: false` — ruled 2026-11-02, `design/rulings/m5b-0c-first-spells-table-2026-11-02.md`).
- **Shop:** the **Add item sheet, creation variant**: "N gp to spend" at the top (dec-10 F-b, cs-21), each buy taken off at once with an Undo toast, **Done shopping** as the one accent button. Kits are **pick rows** on the Gear step (cr-14, mockup creation/06; the row opens what's in the kit and where it lands); a rolled kit's rolls are range-first rows, one tap per roll; a roll made more than once is **one table** ("Roll d4 twice · pick 2 · Weapons", ct3-6, ruled 2026-11-03): the player taps as many rows as rolls, the same row again when the dice show it twice (Twice ✓✓); a full table says "2 of 2 tapped · untap one first."
- **The final check step** is the review screen. It has no override prompt: a choice needing the GM's special approval is flagged there and on the GM's card (cr-7; the earlier "Why? Your GM sees this." is retired; corrected 2026-11-05).
- **To finish card** on the full sheet: "You can play now.", one Tap line per open step; gone when done.

### XP and level up (M7; M7-1, M7-2, ruled 2026-10-28)
- **XP waiting** (GM console, Downtime and the End expedition / End session summaries): one quiet line, "445 XP waiting · Award now"; nothing when the pot is empty.
- **Award XP sheet** (GM; a sheet over the console): the waiting lines (each a Tap line with an edit and a remove action), **+ Bonus XP…** (a reason field), **Who shares** as Chips (the characters who went on the expedition chosen; from M9 retainers marked with their share, "½"; the dead unchosen until tapped), then one row per character: the share as a **tappable Stat** whose tap shows the sum ("127 + 10% prime = 140"), and **Cap** / **Keep** only on a character past the one-level cap. Under the button: "445 waiting + 19 from bonuses". **Award N XP** is the one accent button (the phone action bar on a phone). Empty: "Nothing waiting" and + Bonus XP….
- **Icons** on these screens come from the symbol family (`Icon`) in the ink token, never emoji or font glyphs, and each icon-only button has an accessible name (M7-1 build note: the mockup's ✎ was unreadable in light mode).
- **Player:** a quiet "+N XP" toast; **▲ Ready to level** on the Urgent strip.
- **Level-up preview** (the approved R1 board): **Roll tiles** for the HP roll (up to d10; d12 and bigger show the **Stepper**, typing allowed), What changes, Use / Keep rows, "I'll enter it myself". New abilities carry **Rules ›**; **Learn a spell · Choose ›** opens the Spell picker; a fixed HP gain hides the tiles and states the reason. Confirm before the roll stays tappable and says "Tap your roll first". Several pending levels show one preview after another.
- **Ask the GM** (Downtime-only campaigns, during an adventure): tapping ▲ Ready to level offers **Ask the GM**; the GM's **Waiting list** gets the request with **Allow now** / **Not yet**.

### Spells (M7; M7-3, ruled 2026-10-28)
- **Spells section** (Player screen and full sheet): a **Spell level** per level, "Level 1 · 2 a day", each spell a **Spell line**: the name (opens the rules), duration · range, and **Cast**. Cast spells are crossed out (line and muted text) and sink to the bottom of their level; a lost one reads "lost". "Prepare ›" sits in the section header; after the rest prompt, a small "Prepare spells" button (fl-1).
- **Cast sheet** (only for spells that need something): the reversed form, targets, the timer worked out, the light or condition, then **Cast**. Instant spells skip it and show the "<spell> cast · Undo" toast.
- **Prepare sheet** (N29 follows this): **Same as yesterday** first (one tap, the default); then the slot grid per level, each spell with a count (tap adds one, hold clears it), saved as it changes; book casters see their book, divine casters their class list.
- **Scrolls carried:** each scroll a Spell line with **Read**, and for arcane scrolls **Copy into book…** (a Detail sheet with the pack's copy rule, then **Copied** / **Failed**).
- **Spell rules:** the **Detail sheet**: what it does, at the table, duration and range for this caster, reversed form, source.

### Purse sheet (M7b; M7b-1, ruled 2026-10-28)
- From the coin line on the Player screen; seen only by its owner and the GM. Each denomination in the pack's order; **carried** and each stored place as Treasury rows with **Move** (in an adventure, Move sends a request to the GM); the **Due** list; recent entries as Log lines.
- **Split Loot** keeps the R1 board: "What did you bring back?" filled from the Party stash and claimed loot, each line editable, + Add a valuable; one confirm dialog; then an Undo toast and the log line. A player's copy ends with **Send to the GM** (a draft) until M8.

### Character creation and death (M8b additions)
- When an attached adventure has pre-mades free (not claimed, not dead): **Start from a pre-made** at joining and for a new character, and **Pick a replacement** in the death flow's new-character prompt. Choosing shows the pre-made's sheet details; it fills the guided form, and the GM always approves.

### GM — Downtime
The GM's tabs (see Navigation) plus a **GM inbox**: downtime to resolve, votes to sanction, XP to award, flagged items. The **Campaign** screen holds the GM's settings.

### Campaign (GM) and personal menu
- **Campaign (GM):** the GM's tabs (see Navigation). Rules: first **Rules for this campaign** (B15, ruled 2026-10-25: app settings in fixed topic groups and the packs' optional rules in one Optional rules group, each row with its source tag; since 2026-11-02, lr-7, one folded group per source, the app's settings keeping their rows, and the page stays stacked with Setting rules below, no chips; the only place optional rules are chosen, ruled 2026-10-26; Character fields is a collapsible group there, shown only when the rules name fields, ruled 2026-10-28; see 05), then setting rules (their optional rules read-only, with "Change rules for this campaign"; with more than one, a quiet line "Press and hold a row to move it.", ruled 2026-10-28; a pack whose required pack isn't attached carries a Warning line on its row under its version line, with an **Attach <Other>** small secondary button below it in the page's action row when the library has it, rq-1, see 05) (attach, import, update with a preview; homebrew packs above the rest, each with ⋯ → Export as pack file). Players: players and characters (members, titles, invite, approvals; one line per character naming its player, no player column, ruled 2026-10-26), Companies (⋯ → New company, fiat), Inventory. Still to come: Handouts and maps · **During play** (encounter tables, monster disposition, lantern auto-refill, quiet mode) · Data (packs, JSON export, weekly backup). During play (M8b) adds the adventure switches: Travel moves the clock, shop stock counts, told rumours marked, the active check shown (defaults on; each says what "off" does).
- **Personal menu (everyone):** This device (theme, text size) and the player's own options.

### Fight layouts (M6-8, ruled 2026-10-26, revised 2026-10-29 after the fight dry run; canvas "M6 fight layouts")
- **GM tablet:** a header (Explore / Encounter switch, "Round N · minutes", place and time, Fiat, then ✉ Messages, the open-book Reference, Log and ⋯ as on every GM screen from the M5 gate), then three columns: **Party** (characters and hirelings: HP bar with any bonus HP band, AC, statuses, Down marked quietly, a **Delayed** flag; the recent log at the bottom) · **Foes** (numbered groups, the GM's HP bars, morale score, nudges (a morale trigger is a pop-up over the screen, not a card line; reviewed 2026-11-01), a **More** button for the rest of the reference, ability dots, **+ Status** and **Note** on every card, a player's target shown as "Bram → Bog lurker 1", a dashed card for hidden foes with Reveal) · **Controls** (the turn controls at the top: **End foes' turn / End players' turn**, by whose turn it is (side initiative; reviewed 2026-11-01) or **End turn** per foe or group (individual), with **Next round** as the override; the round-start **which side won** tap only when the setting re-rolls; the round's reference strip; + Add foes; Crit · Fumble and Roll tactics when the pack has those tables; Log and End fight at the bottom; We run is the players' prompt only — foes that run are marked Fled, and a chase is End fight with Undo to reopen it, reviewed 2026-11-01).
- **Several fights** (ruled 2026-10-30, DG15): with a split party, the GM's header gains a **fight switcher** (one chip per fight, named by its group; the running one marked in words); switching pauses the other fight exactly where it was. Players see only their own group's fight.
- **Tapping any combatant** opens its panel over the controls column; party and foes never move. Foes: stats, Damage or heal, behaviour, ability dots, triggers, Mark as, source. Characters and hirelings: class, race and player, HP bar, AC and attack, Damage or heal, statuses with time left, light, saves, spells and abilities, Full character sheet. Close with ✕, a tap outside or Escape; tapping another combatant swaps the panel.
- **GM mirror** (ruled 2026-10-29): tap a foe, then a character: the foe's panel shows its attack and what it needs against that character ("d20 +1 · hits Bram on 14+").
- **Down and the death panel** (ruled 2026-10-29): no pop-up. A character at the setting's threshold shows **Down** quietly on every screen; tapping it opens the **death panel** (the rule's text; with dying rules the rounds so far and when death comes; the setting's buttons, see 05; **Not yet** in its ⋯). Any player or the GM may tap Helped, Healed or Stabilised; only the GM taps Dies (ruled 2026-10-29).
- **GM phone:** the turn controls (End foes' turn, with Next round as the override) sit in the sticky bottom bar (M6-2, revised 2026-10-29).
- **Player phone:** Scene in a fight is one column: the round and whose turn it is, **Foes** first (numbered, "took N damage", statuses, Down; tap one, optional, to see the roll and what hits, which marks it as your target on the GM's screen), the party's HP grid (when the campaign allows it), a log line, then the bottom tabs. On the party's turn the sticky bar holds **End our turn** (side initiative) or **End turn** for your character (individual), (Delay lives on the Player screen only, in individual initiative; reviewed 2026-11-01). **We run** is there too, for any player to call; once called it shows with Undo until the GM resolves it.
- **Player tablet:** Scene in a fight is the B18 table view with the time band reading "Fight · Round N · whose turn" and **two columns, Foes · Party**, in the marching-order band's place, the place folded to its name, prompts in the slot (reviewed 2026-11-01, `design/rulings/m6-fight-screens-review-2026-11-01.md`: the Scene is a reference for players and a display for a common tablet; the You column is gone, and tapping your own character opens the Player screen, which holds everything personal — the attack from a weapon at hand (a pop-up, easily closed, with that attack type's modifiers and foe chips, AC where revealed; a foe tapped on Scene pre-selects), weapons with bonus and damage, spells with Cast, ability dots, At hand with Use and a quiet Pack ›, End turn and Delay in individual initiative only — drawn as screen 5 of `design/mockups/m6/m6-fight-screens.html`, hit points read-only with the bar); a foe on Scene shows its AC once revealed (CS-8); the turn controls, Delay and We run sit as on the phone (2026-10-29); outside fights the Scene drops You.
- **HP bar** (M6-3): segmented, one segment per HP up to 12 and one per 5 HP above (a part-lost segment fills partly), with "N of M"; bonus HP shows as a separate band after it ("+4 bonus"; ruled 2026-10-29); the GM's view of foes, and characters later. Players see "took N damage", "unhurt", statuses and Down (or words, or nothing, per the Combat setting); big groups collapse unhurt foes to "N more unhurt".
- Every state follows the conventions and shows in the gallery: empty, loading, offline, long names; text 14 px at least and 44 px targets. The table test is the real check.

### GM tables and the quick table builder (M6-10; tables dry run, 2026-10-29)
- **Pulling up a table** (aligned with the M5 gate, B2, 2026-11-01): in Explore, Encounter and the fight's Controls column the **Encounter tables ›** row opens the full-screen list and tables; the GM taps the rolled row; no typed roll. *(Earlier: the current table as one row, its name · Roll, its panel opening on tap, ruled 2026-10-29.)* The result applies to the current scene. A place or area's default table is the current one; the GM's pick overrides it. **Table library ›** opens the table library, which is also the way into the prep space.
- **Uses picker:** the registry's groups (Encounters · Fights · Treasure · Places · People · World · Other), collapsible, the campaign's most-used uses first; several may be picked.
- **Quick table builder:** one row per line; a range typed at the line's start ("2-4") sets it; gaps and overlaps show a ▲ warning line and never block saving. Each row opens its extras: count, monster or **New monster…**, nested table, note, tags, GM-only parts, Add foes options. The table's extras: columns, groups, tens-and-units dice.
- A pack table offers **Copy** and **Edit** (kept as is).
- **Tactics:** linked on the monster (its card's More); Roll tactics shows only with a link.

## Key journeys (ruled 2026-10-25)

Replaces the old tap-budget table; its single actions fold into these journeys. Each has three limits: **taps**, **screen changes**, and **stays in context** (every-turn tasks never leave the Explore screen). Each is an automated click-through test that counts taps and screen changes and fails the build when a change goes over budget; journeys whose screens aren't built yet are written as pending tests that switch on when they land.

| # | Journey | Who | Taps | Screen changes | Path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Start a session | GM | 1 | 0 | Start session on the GM console (ruled 2026-10-29, D20: no "Time since last session?" question; the GM moves time in Downtime) |
| 2 | Turn, nothing due | GM | 1 | 0 | Next turn |
| 3 | Turn, a check due | GM | 3 | 0 | Next turn → outcome on the reminder card (→ optional modifier) |
| 4 | Take damage | Player | 1 a point | 0 | On the Player screen (and the full sheet's left column) the HP box's − is one tap per point, applied at once, taps within two seconds folded into one change with one Undo (ruled 2026-11-08, mockup players/04 C); the GM's views keep the damage pad: HP → stepper from the last amount → "Take N damage", 2 taps when the last amount repeats, about 3–5 otherwise (M6-3, ruled 2026-10-26) |
| 5 | Light a torch | Player | 1–2 | 0 | The light's ring over "Light" in At hand (→ "Light…" and the pick if more than one light; lt-2, lt-4) |
| 6 | Give an item (single / stack) | Player | 4 / 5 | 1 | Item → Give → (how many) → person → "Give N to …" |
| 7 | Do an activity (day modes; Act… in turn modes moved to the parking lot, DG12, 2026-10-30) | GM | 4 | 0 | activity → who → outcome |
| 8 | Write a log note | Anyone | 3 + typing | 0 | Log → note box → Add |
| 9 | Show a picture (M8b, with Handouts; D26) | GM | 2 | 0 | Send → Send |
| 10 | End the expedition with a full rest | GM | 3 | 0 | ⋯ → End expedition (summary with Undo, no confirm) → Full rest (ruled 2026-10-29, D22; escape rolls only when a dungeon-exit rule is on) |

**Combined GM turn** (re-budgeted 2026-10-30 after Act… left turn modes; answers after #170): Next turn (1) + the check's Hit or Miss (1) + on a hit the table pick and row (2): **2 taps on a miss, 4 on a hit**, never leaving Explore. Journey 7 stays day modes only.

**M6 journeys** (M6-12; pending tests that switch on when the fight screens land):

| # | Journey | Who | Taps | Screen changes | Path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 11 | A fight round (side mode; revised 2026-10-29) | GM | 1 (2 when the setting re-rolls initiative) | 0 | (which side won →) End foes' turn; a player taps End our turn; the round advances by itself (confirmed 2026-10-29) |
| 12 | Damage a foe (revised 2026-10-29: the GM's job) | GM | pending | 0 | Foe (pre-selected when a player tapped it) → stepper → "Deal N to <foe>" |
| 13 | Add foes from a table row | GM | 2 + stepper | 0 | Encounters row → (how many) → "Add N <foes>" |
| 14 | A morale check | GM | 2 (+1 on a break) | 0 | ▲ Check → They hold / They break (→ All flee / All surrender) |
| 15 | Roll tactics | GM | pending | 0 | Roll tactics → row |
| 16 | End a fight | GM | pending | 0 | End fight → summary → Send loot / Finish without loot (renamed 2026-10-29) |
| 17 | The encounter flow | GM | about 4 (1 straight to Fight) | 0 | Surprise → Reaction → Fight, Talk or Avoid |
| 23 | End our turn (ruled 2026-10-29) | Player | 1 | 0 | End our turn (side) or End turn (individual) |
| 24 | Pick a target (optional) | Player | 1 | 0 | Foe (the roll and what hits show; the GM sees the target) |
| 25 | We run, and Undo | Any player, then GM | 1 to call, 1 to resolve; Undo 1 | 0 | We run → (GM) Got away or Caught; Undo from the toast or the log |
| 26 | The death panel | Player or GM | 2 | 0 | Down character (1 to open) → the panel's button (1 for the action; ruled 2026-10-29) |

Budgets marked pending are set after the paper dry runs (a scripted 5-round fight, target at most one tap per player per round on average; creating GM add-ins). The fight dry run was held on 2026-10-29: journeys 11, 12 and 17 were revised and 23–26 added (numbers 18–22 stay with M7). Journeys 11 and 23–25 were confirmed as written and journey 26 set to 2 taps the same day.

**M7 journeys** (ruled 2026-10-29, E47; pending tests that switch on when the M7 screens land):

| # | Journey | Who | Taps | Screen changes | Path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 18 | Award XP | GM | 3 | 0 | Award now → (one change: a chip or a line) → Award N XP |
| 19 | Level up | Player | 3, no typing | 0 | ▲ Ready to level → HP roll tile → Confirm |
| 20 | Cast a spell | Player | 1 instant, 3 with a sheet | 0 | Cast; or Cast → the sheet's choice → Cast |
| 21 | Prepare with Same as yesterday | Player | 2 | 0 | Prepare › → Same as yesterday |
| 22 | A guided character | Player | about 5–6 minutes | one screen per step | the guided steps (05, Characters) |

## Words and voice (ruled 2026-10-25)

**Links and buttons (ruled 2026-11-03, `design/rulings/m5b-fewer-links-2026-11-03.md` fl-1):** actions are buttons (the one accent; secondary; a **small** secondary in a card's foot); a choice among a few is chips or segments; opening a thing is the whole card or row with ›; **underlined text only goes somewhere** (the back link, a link inside a sentence) or is a help line (Help me choose). Never an action as underlined text; a destructive action is a button with the danger word. Two named exceptions (fl-5, 2026-11-04, `design/rulings/m5b-fewer-links-2-2026-11-04.md`): a **dotted underline** marks a heading you tap to edit or open (the campaign name, the time strip's mode · turn), and the **toast's Undo** is the toast's own control.

The text companion to the component inventory: every component's words follow it. All examples invented.

**Who is speaking**
- **The app speaks plainly, as itself.** It never says "I", never says "please", and never cheers ("Great!", "Awesome", "Oops").
- **Ogham** (the archivist and teacher) speaks only in guided-flow instructions, first-time hints, empty screens, emails, the session sign-off and (when they return) sayings. **Never** in play screens, log lines, buttons, warnings during play, or errors.
- **The user is "you".** Other characters are named: "Wren gave Bram 3 rations."

**Ten rules for every word**
1. **Sentence case** everywhere ("Add item"). The wordmark is the one exception (small capitals).
2. **Buttons start with a verb**, 1–3 words, and name the result ("Add to Backpack", "Send to the GM", "Give 3 to Ilsa"). Never "OK" or "Submit" when a verb fits. A confirm button repeats the question's verb ("Drop 3 torches?" → "Drop"). **Exception:** outcome buttons in a decision prompt may name the outcome instead of starting with a verb ("They hold", "They break", "All flee", "All surrender"; ruled 2026-10-27).
3. **Plain words over jargon.** Game terms come from the setting's own names (a pack's "kindred" stays "kindred"); developer words never appear ("pack", "entry", "field" become "setting rules", "rule", the field's label).
4. **No exclamation marks.** No "successfully" (the change shows), no "simply", "just" or "easy".
5. **Numbers as numerals:** "3 torches", "2-in-6", "5+", "Day 14, turn 3". Units with a space: "60 ft", "24 miles".
6. **Short:** labels ≤ 3 words, notes ≤ 1 line, ⓘ text ≤ 3 sentences.
7. **One name per thing,** everywhere (the glossary below and the component inventory are the reference).
8. **Hidden stays hidden in words too:** never hint at what a viewer can't see ("Charges: ?", "What you notice"; never "This item has hidden charges").
9. **No AI-sounding phrases:** "delve", "embark", "unleash", "journey", "adventure awaits", "Let's…", "Here's…", "dive in", "seamless", "unlock".
10. **Spell it one way:** US English for the app's own text (color, armor); text from a setting pack shows as the pack wrote it.
11. **No middle dot** (ruled 2026-11-07, creation first, the app-wide sweep with the players' screens): a colon after a count ("9 items: leather, spear"), figures as a sentence ("120 rolled, 2 sp spent, 119 gp 8 sp left"), a second line where there is room, commas inside a line; a test fails on " · ". The same for "… ›" links: a row with a chevron or a plain button, never a link ending in ›.
12. **"Pop-up" means a sheet** (ruled 2026-11-07): over the dimmed page, from the bottom on a phone, anchored to its button on a tablet, ✕ or a tap outside closes; a tap outside only closes, it never also taps what lies under it (fix-tap-through, 2026-11-24); never a centred dialog. **"Warning" means the sign**: the triangle with "!" in the warning colour, always with its words.
13. **A name's possessive is always the full 's**, a name ending in s included ("Tess's coin") (wd-1, the lead's, 2026-11-14).
14. **Region, Area, Location** (ruled 2026-11-18, p2p-1, p2p-3, `design/rulings/scene-point-to-point-2026-11-18.md`; on screen since B1, #1000, through `typeWord` and `keyedName`): a place's spots are **Locations** (the pack's `area`), the place that holds them is an **Area** (the pack's `site`), a **Region** stays a Region; "Spots here" becomes "Locations"; the pack format's `type` values do not change. A numbered Location reads "1. The Jetty": the number, a full stop, the name; the full stop is the app's, the name's capitals the pack's.
15. **No explaining lines** (p2p-17, 2026-11-18): a sheet does not explain what the log or Undo will do; the Move sheet's foot line about them goes.
16. **A note says a rule, not how the table runs** (tfw-2, 2026-11-19, `design/rulings/dec-test-feedback-words-2026-11-19.md`): outside character creation, no line reminds the player what the GM gives, checks, reads or decides ("Your GM checks it at the table." is the kind that goes). Character creation is the exception: such lines stay there, where the app teaches more.

**Patterns by component**

| Where | Pattern | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Error | What happened. What to do. | "INT can't be above 18. Lower it to continue." |
| Blocked action (pop-up) | Short title, then why, then the way forward | "Can't cast · Your chainmail stops arcane casting. It ends when you take it off." |
| Confirmation dialog | Question with the verb and object; buttons echo it | "Delete Wren permanently?" · Delete permanently / Cancel |
| Toast | Past tense, what moved, plus Undo | "Dropped 3 torches · Undo" |
| Empty screen | An invitation with the action (Ogham may speak) | "Nothing carved here yet. Add your first character." |
| Guided-flow instruction | One imperative line, about 12 words (Ogham) | "Roll your scores and enter them as rolled." |
| ⓘ explanation | What it is, then what it does at the table; ≤ 3 sentences; source line last | "Magic resistance is added to saves against spells…" |
| Log line | Past tense, actor first, plain facts | "You dropped 3 torches." · "You undid a drop: 3 torches are back with you." |
| Prompt (checks, rolls) | The chance first, then the action | "Roll 1d6 · lost on 1–2" |
| Modifier line | Value, then source | "+1 · Hunter (class)" |
| Status label (screen reader) | Name, then stacks or time | "Exhausted, 2 stacks" · "Blessed, 3 turns left" |
| Option row | Name, then one line of meaning | "Party's lowest · Matches the lowest active character, currently 3." |

**Ogham's voice** (words-and-voice §4, absorbed 2026-10-29)
- **Brief, calm, a little old-world,** never twee. Short sentences; concrete nouns (fire, road, door, ledger).
- **Teaches, never scolds:** "A character needs a name before the GM can see them.", not "You forgot the name."
- **Allowed flavour:** "carved", "kept", "the table", "the road", "the night". **Not allowed:** archaic grammar ("thou", "'tis"), fake ancient quotes, puns in instructions.
